ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U4	The next item is the second reading of the draft law amending the Income Tax Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. For a presentation, please, the Chair of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, Rannar Vassilyev.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U5	The draft law amending the Income Tax Act is numbered 643. Let me briefly present its contents. The content of the draft can be summarised in two points. The first concerns the arrangements for compensation for the use of a private passenger car for work trips. The current possibility of paying a monthly compensation of EUR 60 is lost, for which it was not necessary to prove how this car was used for compensation purposes, i.e. for work trips. The second point concerns the current maximum amount of tax-free compensation, which is currently EUR 256. According to this draft, this limit will rise to EUR 335 per month. The members of the Riigikogu, the committees and the political groups did not submit proposals within the deadline for amendments. A meeting of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, preparing for the second reading of the draft, took place on 3 June. The commission decided to make one amendment to the draft. This proposal concerns the deadline for the entry into force of the law. It was decided to postpone it by two months in order to allow companies to adapt to the changes. The new law may slightly increase the administrative burden on businesses, especially those who used this €64 compensation without the obligation to prove the use of the car. Now it has to be regulated somehow. During the examination of the amendments, the Commission proposed to amend the deadline for the entry into force of the Act by extending it until 1 January 2015. As the draft had been proposed for 1 July as the date of entry into force, two proposals were put to a competitive vote. The competitive vote was won on 1 September and the date was also put to the final vote as an amendment by the Commission. This amendment was then tabled by the Commission. In addition, the debate proposed to further increase the ceiling for compensation to EUR 350 instead of EUR 335. The proposal was not supported by 2 votes in favour, 5 against and 1 abstention. The procedural decisions of the commission were as follows: to put the draft on the agenda of the plenary session of the Riigikogu by 11 June with a proposal to close the second reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U16	Our 19th item on the agenda is the continuation of the second reading of the Penal Code initiated by the Government of the Republic and, in connection with this, the draft law amending other laws 554. ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U17	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The second reading of the draft was likely to take place on 21 May and was interrupted. The deadline for submitting amendments was set at 16 a.m. on 26 May. No proposals were made in the Riigikogu to amend the draft by this deadline. The National Defence Committee of the Riigikogu, the Competition Board, the Confederation of Employers, and the Ministry of Justice and the Ministry of the Interior gave their opinion on the draft. The Committee on Legal Affairs prepared the draft for the continuation of the second reading, which was held at its meeting on 3, 5 and 9 June. In addition to the Ministry of Justice, the Ministry of the Interior and the specialists of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications, the people of the Chamber of Commerce and Industry and representatives of the Confederation of Employers were invited to participate, as well as several sworn advocates. The two main issues addressed were competition crimes and intellectual property offences. When discussing in detail § 400 of the Penal Code, the Committee on Legal Affairs concluded that it is not possible or appropriate at present to change the composition of the crime that it contains. It is not considered right to treat what it says as wrongdoing. The views of the parties differed on this point, but the Commission made its decision in a consensual manner. At the same time, we proposed to the Ministry of Justice that, by 30 September, a complex solution be developed for more effective handling of competition offences. I would like to stress that the Commission has not previously discussed or discussed new solutions to competition crimes in this session either from this point of view, in order to lighten sanctions, as the media, which intervened in the meantime, claimed. The press gave the impression that they wanted to soften the penalties imposed on businesses. Rather, this issue has been in the field of view because the criminal proceedings have not been effective. It's been very time-consuming, the investigations are jammed. In fact, the aim is not to convict an individual or a responsible employee of the company or a senior executive of the company as a criminal offence. The aim is to put companies in charge of large sums of money. The Committee on Legal Affairs intends to further address these issues in the autumn. As I mentioned, there were no amendments on this subject in the Commission. The second issue was intellectual property, which was dealt with by Amendment 3. One important principle was changed. In the past, the qualifying feature for these crimes was a significant scale, i.e. significant damage or significant benefit, which was known to be EUR 4000. The amendment was now proposed to set EUR 200 instead of EUR 4000. So 20 times that amount is going to decrease. One of the new moments in the series of amendments, 28 in total, is proposal 15, with which we intend to amend the law on law enforcement. This law was not previously listed, although the draft 554 is intended to amend 120 laws. If you remember, we had a large law enforcement bill quite recently in this room. There were some gaps in the training process. One of the most questionable principles is the possibility of police intervention. If in some cases the police were able to intervene only in the event of an increased threat, the word 'enhanced' will now be removed from the text by means of a legislative amendment. After all, there are certain irregularities where the police are not allowed to intervene, but no one else, for example, exercising administrative supervision, is obliged to intervene according to the law. One basic explanation is when the police are allowed to enter the dwelling. This is permitted in order to eliminate non-compliance where prolonged or repeated interferences that significantly disturb another person and are not otherwise possible, as well as to counter the imminent increased threat, are spreading beyond the possession of the other person. As I said, the total number of amendments tabled by the Committee on Legal Affairs is 28. We have proposed that the draft be put on the plenary agenda and that the second reading be closed today, on 11 June now it is already 12 June, and that the draft be sent to third reading when the second one is closed, and that the final vote on the draft be carried out.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U33	Our 21st item today is the second reading of the draft law amending the Law on fishing initiated by the Government of the Republic. For a presentation, please address Annely Akkermanni, member of the Environment Committee.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U34	Mr President-in-Office of the Council. Ladies and gentlemen, I'd like to say good night, but I think I have to say good morning. On 17 March 2014, the Government of the Republic initiated a draft law amending the Fisheries Act. Its first reading was completed on 16 April and the deadline for submitting amendments was set at 5 May 2014. No amendments were tabled by members of the Riigikogu, political groups or other committees. The Committee on the Environment finalised three amendments. During the period between first and second readings, the draft has been discussed at two meetings of the Commission. Representatives of the Ministry of Agriculture and the Ministry of the Environment took part in the meeting on 19 May and 3 June. Amendment 1 is based on the desire of coastal fishermen in Pärnu to establish a legal basis for the distribution of the quantities of herring caught by pottrap. The professional fishermen of the municipalities of Häädemeeste and Tahkuranna approached the Riigikogu with the problem that because of the unfair management of herring fisheries, the herring harvest and consequently the income of fishermen are unequally distributed in the Livonian Gulf of Pärnu county. Fishermen reported that in recent years the herring quota in Pärnu County has been common to all fishermen authorised to fish for herring in the Gulf of Riga. When herring catches open, so-called Olympic fishing begins between professional fishermen. Those who are able to catch more herring during the management period will receive a higher income. However, herring reaches different parts of the Gulf of Riga at different times and the Olympic fishery ends some years earlier than the herring has reached all fishermen's fishing grounds. The amendment complements the Fisheries Act so that the Government of the Republic can establish an annual herring yield per cacti. The draft also proposes the possibility of transferring the right to be caught with a water trap and sanctions in case of overfishing. Amendment 2 stipulates that the historic fishing rights of a permanent resident of a small island can only be transferred to a resident of the same small island who, according to the population register, has resided permanently on that island for at least one year. Amendment 3 provides that the Government of the Republic may establish fishing opportunities at sea by sea by water area and for the first time in 2015 for the year 2015 in which the authorised annual catch is fished with a dredge. There are further technical and linguistic changes to the draft, which in no case alter its content. At its meeting on 3 June, the Committee on the Environment adopted the following procedural decisions: to submit the draft for second reading on the plenary agenda of the Riigikogu by 11 June, which became 12 June; to propose that the second reading be stopped and, if it is terminated, to send the draft to the third reading by 18 June, when a final vote could also be taken. That's all I got. Thank you.

When the general public is told about historical fishing rights, everyone thinks about what happened three or four generations ago. In fact, the historic right to catch began in 2002. Shouldn't we change that term?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U55	I cannot say that this question did not arise during the procedure for this draft. Historical fishing rights were established in 2001. The idea was to give fishermen confidence that they had a certain number of fishing gear with which they could fish. On the other hand, this made the situation more difficult, as fishing rights for fish stocks were reduced by cutting the number of 'historical' gears. Historical fishing rights became the subject of trade and there were two aspects of the game. On the one hand, the fisherman had a historic right to fish with a very small number of gillnets. A five-net professional fisherman can't go fishing, but the costs are higher. Another negative aspect that arose is that fish dealers and owners of fishing industries have bought up historical fishing rights and fishermen have become relatively cheap labour. These are the two negative consequences that have emerged from the implementation of the historic fishing right.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U57	I will continue with the questions of Tõnis Kõiv and Erki Arrow. Historical fishing rights were introduced in 2001. The official doctrine of the Estonian state is continuity, i.e. we are the descendants of the country whose existence in 1940 was terminated. Historical fishing rights are known to be based on the collective brigades and the link between the collective farms and, in the case of historical fishing rights, our aim is to maintain the collective farming regime forever. I'm not saying that one year is a surprisingly comical definition from the point of view of history. An Englishman who heard that another traditional festival was going to take place somewhere almost had to fall down dead, another cannot be traditional! And one year isn't history. Am I right?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U58	Yes, Mr. Gräzin, you are absolutely right. Thank you very much for this reply. Indeed, when it comes to fishing with a pottrap, historical law is based on collective farms. You're absolutely right.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U86	Our last item, No 24, is the second reading of the draft law amending the Waste Act initiated by Inara Luigas, Kalle Laaneti, Lembit Kaljuvee and Rainer Vakra. I would like to present a chair of the Vice-Chairman of the Environment Committee, Erki Noole.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U87	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable people in the hall! The draft law amending the Waste Act was initiated by Riigikogu members on 19 June 2013. The first reading was closed on 18 September last year and the deadline for submitting amendments was set at 2 October 2013. Seven amendments were tabled by the Centre Party Group; the Committee on the Environment itself also finalised seven amendments. The draft has been discussed by the Commission on several occasions. The first time was 12 December last year. The next time there was a debate on 17 March, then on 21 April, 24 April and 3 June. On 27 May, there was also a meeting on the future of waste management, where representatives of the cooperation structures between the local authorities and the three regional authorities were invited. All these meetings were explained by representatives of the Ministry of the Environment. The proposals for amending the Waste Act are as follows: The first proposal is to delete from § 1 (1) of the draft, which repeals § 66 (11) of the Waste Act. According to this, the shipment of waste may be organised in such a way that the sole customer of the undertaking carrying out the waste and the paying agent is a local government unit or a non-profit organisation authorised by the latter. In such a case, the obligation to keep records of and settle waste holders shall be incumbent on the local government unit or the latter's authorised non-profit association. Paragraph 67 (1) is supplemented by a sentence as follows: 'In the last case, the administrative territories of the cooperating local authorities may form a single transport area, taking into account the number of inhabitants of the region set out in paragraph 5 of this section. ' Amendment 3: In accordance with the first sentence of § 67 (1) of the Waste Act, the local government unit organises a service concession independently or in cooperation with other local government units in order to find the operator of the waste transport service, in accordance with the provisions of the Public Procurement Act. Amendment 4: in accordance with § 71 (2) (2) of the Waste Act, the waste management code to be established by the local government council provides for "the prevention of risks to health and the environment from waste or, where this is not possible, reduction measures, including regular movements of municipal waste from the densely populated area at least once every four weeks and from the distributed area at least once every 12 weeks'. Paragraph 1 (3) of the draft law complements § 71 (2) (2) of the Waste Act with the provision that the regular transport of municipal waste is permitted at least once every 12 weeks, in addition to the diffuse settlement area, in the densely populated area, where bio-waste treatment at the site is possible. The implementation of this supplement depends on the choice of each local government unit. The amendment drafted by the Committee on the Environment clarifies the wording of subsection 71 (2) (2) of the Waste Act in such a way that the text of point 2 is formulated in two sentences and replaces the word 'treatment' with 'composting' and 'potential' with 'guaranteeed'. Amendment 5 The Commission proposes to supplement § 1 (4) of the draft, supplementing § 71 (2) 21 of the Waste Act on the laying down of composting requirements for bio-waste in the waste management rules to be established by the municipal council. The inclusion of such a provision in the Waste Act allows the local government to implement the provision in section 1 (3) of the draft, which, if the composting requirements for bio-waste are met at the site of the waste, will allow the local authorities to reduce the transport of municipal waste to a single time in the densely populated area within 12 weeks. Amendments No 6 to 8 In the event of the entry into force of § 1 (1) of the draft law, the principle of legitimate expectations should be taken into account in the case of local government entities which currently carry out the shipment of waste pursuant to § 66 (11) of the Waste Act. Therefore, on the proposal of the Committee on the Environment, the Waste Act is supplemented by § 1366 on the validity of waste transport contracts, according to which for the implementation of § 66 (11) of the Waste Act, it is valid for contracts concluded in accordance with § 2 of the Bill until 1 October 2014 (Article 1 (5) of the draft). The Act is also supplemented by Section 1368 on the implementation of Section 66 (11) of the Waste Act, according to which pending public procurement procedures and contractual procedures for the implementation of Section 66 (11) of the Waste Act are completed until 1 October 2014 and contracts concluded in these procedures are valid until their expiry date (Article 1 (7) of the draft). In addition, the draft is supplemented by § 1367 of the Waste Act on the frequency of shipments of waste, as proposed by the Environment Committee. According to this, the concession contracts for the shipment of waste concluded before 1 October 2014 are valid until the expiry of their term of validity with the frequency of the shipment of waste provided for in the contracts (Article 1 (6) of the draft). Amendment 9 proposes that the Committee on the Environment amend the draft by § 2 and regularise the draft on 1 October 2014. This period of entry into force shall allow sufficient time for all persons and bodies concerned to examine and implement the law. On 5 May this year, a working version of the draft was sent to the heads of all local government units and invited them to participate in the debate on the draft on 27 May, including submitting their amendments. Technical and linguistic changes have also been made to the draft, which in no case alter the content of the draft. At its meeting on 3 June, the Committee on the Environment decided to submit the draft for second reading on the plenary agenda of the Riigikogu by 11 June, to propose that the second reading be stopped and, when the second reading is completed, to send the draft to the third reading. Some of the procedural moments. The Committee on the Environment asked both the Committee on Constitutional Affairs and the Legal and Analytical Department of the Riigikogu for an opinion on the compliance of the draft with the Constitution and the amendments and effects resulting from the entry into force of the Act. In general, the draft is in line with the Constitution, Silver Sära, adviser to the Legal and Analytical Department, responded. There was also no apparent contradiction with the European Charter for Local Government. The state has the right to decide which rules local governments choose the waste shipper. However, it was noted that § 67 (1) should mention the reasons for limiting the formation of transport areas, if any. In addition, implementing provisions should be included in the draft. That is what the Committee on the Environment has done. The Committee on Constitutional Affairs pointed out that § 1 (2) of the draft does not make clear whether the establishment of joint waste transport areas must also take account of the limit of 30000 inhabitants laid down in § 67 (5) of the Waste Act or whether the corresponding regulation allows the formation of an undefined transport area. In addition, it is not clear from the draft whether, if the transport contracts concluded with the carrier found in cooperation with the municipality cease to exist, the joint waste management area of the municipalities will also cease to be valid. They proposed to clarify these issues in the course of further processing of the draft. The Committee on Constitutional Affairs did not see any interference with the Constitution. So much more briefly that the procedure for this draft has given rise to relatively many emotions. Most local authorities do not support this, but some individuals did. I am now arbitrarily suggesting that about 30 local authorities did not support the draft, four or five supported it. It was possible to reduce the frequency of transport to 12 weeks that gave rise to much discussion. Mr Aivar Riisalu has already asked about this in the debate on the previous draft legislation on waste. I will explain that local authorities are only entitled to allow the frequency of shipments of waste to be reduced if composting is ensured. That's all for me right now.

This approach to legislation is wrong, and when voting on the same draft, we should first and foremost consider whether local authorities have the right to provide certain services or not. That's it.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U170	Good night, ladies and gentlemen. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I was motivated by the fact that, on the one hand, it seems to me that some of the questioners did not quite understand what they were asking for, and that the Commission's rapporteur also answered his personal assessments in the way that early morning thoughts brought him into his mouth. We have been working on this bill for almost a year. The draft was initiated when Inara Luigas, Rainer Vakra, Kalle Laanet and Lembit Kaljuvee were members of the opposition. We have repeatedly organised round tables and various other debates within the framework of this draft. It has been argued here that in one debate everyone opposed the draft. On this point, I can say that, as chairman of the committee, I invited all those who had previously informed the Commission that they were against this draft. It would have been a miracle if someone at the beginning of this round table had taken a supportive position. There are two very fundamental proposals in the proposal. Firstly, it should result in a lower price; the second result is environmental friendliness. First of all, I'm gonna say a few words about them. There is a need to address a situation where, since the entry into force of the municipal waste transport regime, people's interest in collecting waste by type has been reduced and reduced. We all know this question to sort out when we have to fill the garbage disposal by the end of the month. It is now mandatory to have a waste bin filled in four weeks. In order to continue to value the sorting of municipal waste, local governments will have the opportunity to extend the delivery range to 12 weeks from autumn when we adopt this law. This procedure is developed by each municipality itself. This gives the municipality, the municipality manager, the city, the city manager the opportunity to reduce the waste collection bills specifically for people who sort waste and compost bio-waste. The possibility of having the container emptied less often means real savings for everyone. Garbage transport gets cheaper, ends with the end of every month it's reasonable to cross-cross a container with all kinds of stuff, because it'll be emptied at the end of the month anyway. Change number two. I am pleased to quote Kalev Kallot, who in the course of the debate asked why something had to be changed. He lives in Haabersti and is satisfied with the service because he only pays 3.60 a month. A hundred percent agree! The primary condition of this draft is that nothing changes in this sense, that prices will continue to be understandable and that they will emerge as a result of the competition. All companies, including those created by the municipal authorities, can participate in the competition. For example, Kihnus is a municipal company that also participated in the competition. But when a municipal monopoly is set up, it certainly does not mean a cheaper price. I am surprised by some people's conviction that municipal monopoly or any monopoly automatically means a lower price. The law must protect the person, not the other way around. The current law's hole allows non-citizen municipalities to create a waste disposal monopoly and to set prices that are not subject to any market logic. This is a hidden trash tax that I, as a resident, cannot refuse. The purpose of this draft is to put an end to all kinds of Soviet heritage. At that time, too, the State set prices at its own discretion. An artificially established municipal monopoly with opaque pricing must not be the future of Estonian waste management. It is important to stress here that, if you wish, all municipalities can still organise the transport of municipal waste independently, in cooperation with the neighbouring municipality, for example by authorising a non-profit association or foundation. I repeat: the only change is that a sub-unit of a municipality cannot charge the residents a commission to cover its activities. Today, Tallinn is only in Northern Tallinn to implement this system, but the intention is to extend it over the capital. The form of cooperation without hidden garbage tax is used elsewhere in Estonia, be it Valgamaa, Hiiumaa and Kohtla-Järve, for example. A good example is the Centre for Waste Management in Central Estonia, with 28 municipalities involved. All these municipalities can manage without the application of the garbage tax, without additional revenue from the commissioning activities. Two golden rules must apply to ideal waste management. First, whoever pollutes pays, and second, when the head takes, the wallet gets a break. There has been a lot of talk here about who has been the ones who have supported this bill. I repeat: NGO Kesk-Estonian Waste Management Centre, all of Valgamaa, in addition to the Estonian Owners' Confederation, the Estonian Employers' Confederation and the Estonian Waste Operators' Association. We have 215 municipalities and there are those where cooperation is carried out, where non-profit organisations are implemented, all over Estonia. For them, the change basically means nothing more than giving municipalities more flexibility to support people who sort, compost and behave in an environmentally friendly way. A fundamental change is the abolition of monopoly municipal mediation, which selects the waste manager on the one hand, the carrier on the other, and adds development costs to the account. This development cost should not have been the result of sucking a pen in the private office, but thanks to the competition. The local government's own companies can also participate in the competition. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U171	Valeri Korb, please! Three minutes extra time.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U172	Ladies and gentlemen, Despite the fact that the time is quite late and the sun is soon to rise, I would like to talk about a few thoughts about this bill that we have been discussing with you here for a long time. The draft under discussion only deals with a marginal part of the waste law and everything would be fine if this draft did not appear to be the active lobby of the waste shippers, which would result in a breach of the autonomy of the municipalities. The draft proposes repealing Paragraph 66 (11) of the Waste Act. Under this paragraph, the shipment of waste may be organised in such a way that the sole customer of the undertaking carrying out the waste and the paying agent is a local authority or a non-profit organisation authorised by the latter. In such a case, the obligation to keep records of and settle on the holders of waste shall lie with the local government or a non-profit organisation authorised by the latter. According to § 6 (1) of the Local Government Organisation Act, one of the local government units is responsible for managing waste management in the municipality or city if this task is not assigned by law to anyone else. In a situation where the local government takes over the provision of services and the conclusion of a contract with waste holders, the local government has undertaken to carry out the management of waste under the Local Government Ordinance Act. The draft has been analysed by the lawyers of the Soraainen Office, who considered that the draft constituted an intensive interference with the autonomy of the local government. In their opinion, the draft explanatory memorandum does not indicate whether less stringent means have been considered to achieve the objectives, which would guarantee the protection of consumers' rights and, at the same time, would not seriously undermine the autonomy of the local government or break the functioning of waste management. The repeal of Paragraph 66 (11) of the Waste Act may result in the competitiveness of small and medium-sized enterprises being undermined in the interests of large companies. The Supreme Court has explained that companies have no constitutionally protected right to require the state to intervene in order to protect them from excessive competition and to ensure their market share. A solution has now been found in section 66 (11) of the Waste Act, where the local government provides direct service to its customers, which would apply in Tallinn, for example. In addition to the city of Tallinn, a considerable number of local authorities wish to choose this solution, and we know that. The fact that the local government provides waste transport services as a vital service is a common practice throughout Europe. In the Nordic countries, local authorities predominantly provide this service by purchasing the waste transport service and owning the facility. In the rest of Europe, the waste transport service is provided through the local government's own companies. This draft aims to end this European approach to waste management in Estonia. The Centre Party Group cannot support the draft in this form. For us, the autonomy of the municipality is very important. The level of waste management in Estonia has also steadily increased in the last five years, so there is no need to break down the current system. In addition, draft 668 on the implementation of the General Part of the Environmental Code Act also provides for the amendment of the Waste Act. The Centre Party Group therefore proposes that the second reading be suspended. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U173	Mikhail Stalnukhin, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U174	Mr President! We would have drowned in trash at the time if Minister of the Environment Villu Reiljan hadn't arrived. We did everything we had to do. Oh, how many problems there have been with this sorting station over the last few years! This year only ended the time when we had to report every year on its use. And the second argument: if our colleagues were qualified garbage disposal managers, their talk would affect me, but, as we know, they are not. So leave the Waste Act alone, let it be dealt with by the appropriate authorities! Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U243	We all remember the time when the opening up of the market caused great fears. The electricity price is currently at a completely comparable level to the price it had on a regulated market a few years ago. Electricity is considerably cheaper in Estonia than in Latvia and Lithuania, thanks to very good connections. The question was what the next steps of the government could be. The most important steps from Estonia's point of view have been taken by building Estlinks, but our interest is certainly to ensure security of supply and a competitive price for our neighbours as well. The following project is therefore the construction of the third electricity line in the Latvian direction. On the one hand, it means greater energy security for Latvians and, on the other hand, the possibility of obtaining cheaper electricity, but thirdly, it also means that Estonian electricity producers can sell more electricity towards Latvia. ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U246	Thank you. Excise duty accounts for a very small proportion of the total figures for natural gas. I am convinced that in every country, therefore in Estonia, both tax policy and general government policy must encourage the use of more renewable energy sources alongside fossil fuels, energy sources that leave a smaller ecological footprint on the environment. I repeat, the effect of raising gas excise duties on the gas bill is very small, but this change of message may still send that families who still choose the source of fuel when they set up their home would seriously wonder whether they would choose a fossil fuel, be it gas or diesel oil. The Road Technology Centre has examined the long-term cumulative sub-financing for the repair of state roads and has received an amount of EUR 836 million. For local roads and streets, the cumulative underfunding is broadly EUR 300 million. As a result, the road surfaces of the main Estonian roads are on average 13 years old, the road surfaces of the main roads are on average 25 years old and the road surfaces of the secondary roads and local roads are already more than 30 years old. In these circumstances, come and tell us that the right thing to do is to reduce the amount of road maintenance is foolish. The hope that more excise duties will be received each year is not true. I will give you an example: in 2013, 417 million fuel excise duties were received in the budget, and 402 million were planned this year, so 15 million less. There's a place to think. Now, hurriedly, in the hours of the night, adopt such a law of the law of the law, in my opinion, that would be foolishness. In order to prevent this nonsense, the Group of the Estonian Centre Party proposes to reject the draft law amending the Road Act at first reading 681.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U378	Let's go! We will begin the first reading of the draft law amending the Electricity Market Act initiated by the Committee on Economic Affairs. Commission waitress Kalle Palling, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U379	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The Estonian electricity market opened for all consumers on 1 January 2013. For the efficient functioning of the market and the promotion of competition, a digital data exchange platform was set up to exchange data on the electricity market for the exchange of open suppliers, for the transmission of metering data and for the fulfilment of the obligations imposed on the market participant by law and for the enforcement of the rights conferred on it. The obligation to set up a data exchange platform was imposed on the system operator, but there is now a need to clarify the rules for covering the administrative costs of the data exchange platform. The transposition of the Renewable Energy Directive introduced an obligation for the transmission system operator to issue certificates of origin for electricity produced from renewable energy sources and to establish a database for their issuance. In order to join the trading system allowing international transactions, it is necessary to specify in the Electricity Market Act the requirements for the operations to be carried out in the certificate of origin. In the proceedings of the Riigikogu, the draft law amending the Electricity Market Act 318 is the result of which further processing is that the European Commission has not yet granted us the relevant State aid permit. MKM has recently exchanged information closely with the Competition Directorate of the European Commission, but it is likely that we will not be granted a State aid permit before the Riigikogu's summer vacation. At the same time, there are 318 points which are not directly linked to the State aid authorisation, but which will need to be implemented in the near future. The draft law amending the Electricity Market Act at first reading 687 today reflects the financing of the data exchange platform and the requirements for operations on certificates of origin for electricity produced from renewable energy sources. The relevant provisions were originally set out in the text of draft 318. The Committee on Economic Affairs of the Riigikogu discussed the initiation of the draft law amending the Electricity Market Act at its meeting on Tuesday, 3 June. Timo Tatar, Head of the Energy Department of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications, and Siim Meeliste, Head of the same department, had been invited to the meeting, who provided an overview of the reasons for initiating the draft and provided further explanations. The Commission decided to initiate the draft by 7 votes in favour, 1 against, 1 abstention, to appoint a rapporteur here, to propose that the draft be put on the agenda for the plenary session on 10 June, to close the first reading and to set the deadline for the amendments at 17 June. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U402	We will move on to the third item on the agenda: Aivar Riisalu, Arto Asa, Deniss Borodichi, Inara Luigas, Kalev Lillo, Kalle Laaneti, Lauri Laasi, Lauri Luige, Lembit Kaljuvee, Olga Sõtnik, Peeter Laurson, Rainer Vakra, Rannar Vassiljev, Remo Holsmer, Valeri Korb and Vladimir Velman, first reading of the Maritime Safety Act Amendment 646. I am pleased to welcome a member of the Riigikogu, Deniss Borodichi, in the speech chair of the Riigikogu.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U403	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, I start with the fact that the current version of the Maritime Safety Act does not indicate the distinction between the calculation of the waterway charge and the replacement of a ship on a regular service with another ship. The current regulation of the waterway charge is based on a "ship-based approach' and therefore, in the event of replacement of a ship, the rate to be charged by another ship shall be taken into account. In view of the interest of the State in the continuation of scheduled journeys and the interest of shipowners in a more flexible calculation of the waterway charge, as well as the fact that in certain cases, for reasons beyond the control of shipowners, there may be a need to replace the ship, the existing legislation needs to be supplemented. Let us see, then, what are the three aspects with which we want to supplement the law. First, to keep a common record of visits in case of replacement of a vessel. Secondly, the principle that the visits due are to be based on a ship with a higher charge of water for the whole calendar year will apply. And the third point: there will be a possibility of offsetting if a lower rate of the waterway charge has been taken into account before the replacement of the vessel, but the above-mentioned principle of the higher rate of the charge would be that of a higher rate of the waterway charge. The draft Law on Supplementing the Maritime Safety Act also sets out the principle that only ships of the same ship are subject to differences in the calculation of the waterway charge when a ship is replaced. Replacing a ship with another vessel can only be considered if the shipowner is the same. One of the shipowners carries out regular voyages and, if, in the course of its normal operation, there is a need to replace a ship, it is justified to make a distinction as regards the calculation of the waterway charge. Therefore, in the case of a new wording, the ship-based approach is changed to a regular line approach in the calculation of the visits due when the ship is replaced, but the other principles of the calculation of the waterway charge are not changed. For example, visits on a larger ship still require higher fees. The view of the calendar year is important in this context, as the number of visits leading to the reduction of the fee may be fulfilled during the first months of the year. If, in this situation, a ship is replaced after the number of visits has been completed, the keeping of records during the calendar year shall ensure that the waterway charge is set off. As I have already mentioned, in this context, it is no longer important whether a ship is replaced temporarily or permanently by a clear rule laying down the principle of a higher rate of waterway charge. Maybe a little internship. In this respect, it should be noted that the replacement vessel has so far been introduced by only one undertaking, but there is more than one undertaking requesting the replacement vessel to be used. However, since there is a ship-based approach to the calculation of the waterway charge so far, the possibility of using a replacement vessel has not been used any more. At the same time, there is a need and a desire. Please support the bill!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U404	Good waitress, thank you! There are no questions. I would like to ask Kalle Palling, member of the Economic Commission, to present the speech chair of the Riigikogu.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U405	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. The Ministry's officials drew the Commission's members' attention to the fact that the proposed amendment to the draft requires restructuring of the Maritime Administration's information system, which cannot be carried out without a certain period of preparation. The initiator of the draft act explained that, for reasons of legal clarity, the draft proposal seeks to clarify one of the sections of the Maritime Safety Act relating to the replacement of ships on scheduled services. The amendment aims to avoid a situation where someone interprets the law in such a way that the ship owner or the ship agent serving the regular service is obliged to pay double the fee when replacing the ship. The initiator of the draft act explained that the Ministry of Economic Affairs also acknowledges that such a situation could indeed arise, and since the Ministry has more experience in this field, it has proposed in the Government's opinion a better wording of the draft. The initiator of the draft act was in agreement with this wording and since the draft could still be adopted as a law during the spring session, it was proposed that the draft be sent to the first reading as amended. The Committee on Economic Affairs decided to propose that the draft be placed on the agenda of the plenary session on 10 June, that it be submitted for first reading in a modified form with the consent of the initiator, as was the previous one, that it be proposed to terminate the first reading as well as to give a consensus decision 5 p.m. That too was a consensual decision. ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U407	We will move on to the fourth item on the agenda, which is the first reading of the draft law amending the Law on excise duties on alcohol, tobacco, fuel and electricity initiated by the Government of the Republic and the Law on the Special Labelling of Liquid Fuel. I ask the Minister of Finance Jürgen Ligi of the Riigikogu speech chair!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U408	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! The draft law amending the Law on excise duties on alcohol, tobacco, fuel and electricity and the Law on special labelling of liquid fuels raises the rates of excise duties on alcohol and tobacco in order to increase the revenue of the state budget and to limit the consumption of products harmful to health. Alcohol excise duty will be increased by 15% next year, but by 10% every year during the period from 2016 to 2018. The excise duty on cigarettes, smoking and chewing tobacco will be increased by 5% in three years from 2016. In order to facilitate the use of biogas used as heating fuels, the excise duty exemption for this commodity will be introduced while the excise duty on natural gas used as heating fuels will be increased by 20% in 2015, 2016 and 2017. The share of excise duty in the price of natural gas is very low and the price of natural gas to domestic customers increases slightly by more than 1% per year, by 1.4% for the eligible consumer. The micro-producer households and housing associations of electricity, which are connected to the grid and whose equipment has a capacity of less than 100 kW, will subsequently be exempted from excise duty on the consumption of self-produced electricity. This will create additional incentives to consume renewable energy. The use of specialty diesel fuel will be permitted only in agriculture and fishing with fishing vessels in Estonia. The total exemption from excise duty will disappear. It is also convenient to put an end to the tax credit for light fuel oil. It is consumed very little in heating houses, as it is an expensive form of heating. However, the temptation to use this fuel in diesel engines is great, for there it provides considerable savings due to diesel excise. The amendment is also in line with European environmental and energy policies. Buildings must focus on savings, insulation, and also change the form of heating, both of which we plan investment grants. As a result of the completion of the special marking of light fuel oil, only one special-marked fuel will remain in use in Estonia: special-purpose diesel. In rail transport and shipping in Estonian waters, the right to use specially labelled fuel will disappear from 2015 onwards, and for heating and electricity generation, special-purpose diesel can be used until 1 May 2015, that is to say, the end of the heating period of this year. The financing plan for the draft National Budget Strategy 2015-2018 provides, in particular, for an increase in public transport costs of EUR 5 million. In the heating sector, support measures are to be financed from other sources. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U409	Thank you, Minister. You have a lot of questions. Kalev Kallo, please!

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U495	Ladies and gentlemen, The draft law 682 was discussed twice at the meeting of the Committee on Monetary Affairs: first on 3 June and second on 9 June. I will give a brief overview of what has happened in these sittings. Mr Jürgen Ligi, Minister of Finance, Mr Marek Uusküla, Head of the Department of Customs and Excise Policy, and Mr Lauri Lelumes, Chief Specialist, also attended the meeting on 3 June. I will mark the issues that have been the subject of the debate and try to give a brief overview of the debate on them. Firstly, the rates of excise duty on natural gas in Estonia compared to other countries of the European Union were discussed. It was generally stated that they were below the EU average, but since no precise comparison could be provided by the Ministry, the Committee on Monetary Affairs was asked to provide an overview of the rates of excise duties on natural gas in the European Union countries. This request was granted by the Ministry and it can be concluded that the rate of excise duty on natural gas in Estonia is now about twice that in the European Union on average. The discussion shows a comparison with Latvia and Lithuania, I might add that this rate is about four times lower than in Finland. One issue was the use of specially labelled diesel, specifically in which areas this fuel will remain. It can continue to be used in agriculture and, in part, in the fisheries sector, according to the draft submitted to us. The questions that had already been discussed were whether the specific labelling of fuel could stop at all and what debate had been held on the subject. The main problem child in this respect is the agricultural sector. It has been stated that special fuel regulation could be replaced by direct payments. But as far as I know, in the case of direct payments, the problem is that they cannot be differentiated and losers would be producers whose production is more energy intensive than those who do not expect very high fuel consumption. That is why this substitution is problematic. There was also the difference between the excise duties on strong and lean alcohol. This debate has also been held here today. For the most part, there was a debate in the Commission with the same content, so I will not focus on it for long. The main question was the proportion of the excise duty on beer and spirits, but the problem of these sweet drinks was also addressed. A somewhat specific question was the measurement of the semi-product of oil shale fuel oil, to which answers were given. It was examined whether biogas was currently exempt from excise duty or taxed. A representative of the Ministry of Finance said that until the end of last year, the Tax and Customs Board classified biofuels as natural gas because it was the closest product. However, the Member States of the EU qualify it under another commodity code. In the European Union's product classification working group, the case was finally assessed and biogas obtained a different product code from natural gas. This obscured the current excise law for the Estonian Tax and Customs Board so that biogas was no longer taxed. For reasons of legal clarity, the draft states clearly that biogas is not subject to taxation. The tax exemption for ewe gas, i.e. oil shale or peat or wood, was also discussed. It was examined why this was not provided for. There was also an interest in the biofuel target. Again, from a somewhat more detailed area, the issue of the tax liability of the housing association of the household consumer producing electricity using the capacity of 100 kW and the equipment connected to the system was under discussion. The principle is that regardless of whether a device is connected to a network or not, there is no tax liability. In the case of electricity supplied to the network, the network operator shall be liable for tax. When dealing with excise duties on alcohol, a link with the consumer price index was identified. It was asked whether the planned increase in excise duty was in line with the consumer price index. The initiator of the bill stated that, above all, the aim is not to increase the availability of alcohol, and this means that it is not wise to take the consumer price index as a reference base, but rather to take a person's income. Special fuel compensation in the transport sector was also at issue. According to the plans, it lies ahead in passenger transport; it does not concern freight transport. As a result, the impact of the change on competitiveness was also raised. It should be noted that when compared with one of our main competitors in Latvia, which has been somewhat more successful in rail transport than in Estonia, the fuel used by the railway in Latvia is not at a preferential position in terms of taxation, but the question is how the charges for the use of railway infrastructure are divided between passenger traffic and freight transport. There was also a question of ferry traffic, and the Ministry of Finance said that there was extra money in the budget to prevent tax changes from being passed on to prices. In the Commission, as was the case today, the Chancellor of Justice's attention to the sharp increase in the price of light fuel oil and the fact that people who are currently using this type of heating may not be able to switch to a new source of heating within the time envisaged. On this point, the representatives of the Ministry of Finance said that this is one of the reasons why the entry into force of this point has been postponed to the end of the next heating period. The actual price increase would reach people during the next heating period. As it was also complained that there was too little time to examine the documents, it was agreed that the draft would be sent to the first reading by 10 June, but if necessary there would be a possibility to ask further questions about the draft next Monday, 9 June. It was decided to propose to the Bureau that the draft be put on the agenda of Tuesday 10 June. There were 6 members of the Commission in favour, 2 against and no abstentions. As a result of the Commission's agreement on 3 June to continue the debate on 9 June, it also took place. There was a debate on whether or not the Minister should be present. He was present at the first debate. In general, this meeting of the Commission also focused on a substantive debate. Let me give a brief overview of the issues that came up in the second session. The increase in excise duty on cigarettes was clarified, in particular in 2016. According to the draft of the Ministry of Finance, the annual increase is 5%. At the same time, the members of the Commission argued that somewhere there was an impression that the actual increase in excise duty should be 10%. The draft is limited to 5%. Once again, the subject was specially marked fuel and rail. I would not dwell on this in any longer, as the content of the debate overlapd relatively much with that of the debate in the first sitting. For example, the abolition of the excise duty on biogas and the need for a State aid authorisation were again discussed. It was said that, indeed, a State aid authorisation was needed for this purpose. This item was last on 9 June in the plenary session and the time for the closure of the session is set out very precisely in the Rules of Procedure and Home Affairs Act at 1 p.m. It has to be said that there were more questions than the time format allowed. Sadly, everyone who wanted to ask questions could not do so. However, our procedural decisions needed a decision, because earlier it had been proposed to put the draft on the plenary agenda by 10 June. It was decided to propose that the first reading should be completed by 6 members of the Commission, against 4 to set the deadline for amendments at 5.15 p.m. on 17 June, with 6 people in favour and 4 against, and to appoint Rannar Vassilyev, the chairman of the committee, as rapporteur, as well as 6 members of the Commission and 4 against. Before I start answering questions, I will say that, in fact, we had this draft, not in terms of content, but in terms of procedure, partly during the debate at the committee meeting yesterday. We decided that due to the expiry of the time limit and because it was not possible for everyone to ask as many questions as they wanted, we would discuss it further in our Thursday session. Then the questions that have not been asked in the Chamber could be raised and answered in the Commission. Indeed, there is a limited number of questions and answers in this Chamber. At the same time, we also raised the question of the deadline for amendments, as is the case here today for another draft and another committee. In general, the Riigikogu's practice has shown that ten days as a deadline for amendments is not appropriate, and so the question has arisen as to when to deviate from it. We discussed this very long in committee and decided that the chairman of the committee would be empowered to raise this issue the next time the chairmen of the committees met. Thank you.

The first sentence of the relevant paragraph shows the nature of what is currently happening: 'The draft has not been prepared in advance due to the urgent need to implement the Government of the Republic's action plan. ' Ladies and gentlemen, that explains what we are doing here today. What is being set out here today is not a goal. It is actually a means of achieving some other goals. The whole process that is going on here is not a goal, it is a tool. And what are the goals, that's a separate subject. So let us talk about the provisions that are being sought to enforce. First, alcohol duty. We have already agreed on this agreement a certain number of years ago and will be passed on to 2015 and 2016 with the aim of increasing the excise duty rate on alcohol by 5% per year in four consecutive years. This is a valid law. It is now foreseen that the future law should increase 15% in 2015 and not much has been said, in all three following years, by 1.5% in 2016, 2017 and 2018. Today we have talked mainly only about next year. For me, the question is, if that's the way to ask, what is the right percentage? What's that supposed to come from? The minister answered my question evasively. Since I brought in the Conjuncture Institute, he said we don't have to ask the Conjuncture Institute about it, he has some data of his own. What kind of information did you miss these answers from? But I'd like to know where that light came from. In previous years, on the basis of the database used by the Minister, it has been concluded that 5% is correct, but now it says that 15% and 10% in the future are correct. One thing I can say: surely we should take the view that alcohol will not become cheaper over time. We will take the income we receive, or the average wage or median wage, whatever the notional figure, and compare it with the price of alcohol. In this way, when converted, alcohol should certainly not become cheaper. By the way, I can say that I did some tests, and I have to say that the 5% increase that was agreed is very likely to result in a situation where alcohol becomes cheaper over time. Therefore, we should increase the rate of excise duty. The question is, what is the correct percentage: either the exponential increase of 15% at the 5% agreed so far or something else. It seemed to me that the calculation showed 7 to 8% after we left. Thus, the increase in alcohol duty is not based on anything other than what the draft explanatory memorandum says: we need budgetary resources to achieve our specific objectives. ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U590	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The Finance Committee discussed the draft Act amending the Tax Administration Act and the VAT Act, initiated this year on 2 June, at its meeting on 3 June and 9 June. At its meeting on 3 June, the Minister of Finance Jürgen Ligi gave an overview of the objectives of the draft, and on 9 June, the members of the Commission were answered by the staff of the Ministry of Finance Lemmi Oro and Anneli Valgma. Four issues can be highlighted as the main objectives of the draft. Most of them were also discussed in advance with the Minister, but I will still list them. First, the extension of the initial period for checking the refund claims to 60 calendar days; second, the publication on the website of the Estonian Tax and Customs Board of the amount of state taxes paid by taxable persons; third, if the circumstances which alter the tax liability are not identified during the review, the revision will be prepared only at the request of the taxable person; fourth, the tax debt relating to the estate will not be counted as interest from the opening of the inheritance. I will now give an overview of the discussions that have taken place in the Commission. They asked why the repayment period was extended. As the Minister explained in his recent presentation, this amendment concerns, in particular, cross-border transactions and the need to combat tax fraud. The current time limit for the initial verification does not allow the tax authorities to establish sufficiently whether the claim for reimbursement is justified, i.e. whether the procedure should be extended or not. The extension of the withdrawal period will allow for a more thorough verification of the reimbursement claims at a very high financial risk. They asked whether one of the thoughts of going from 30 to 60 days was that the state should not pay interest. The Minister has given a reasoned reply on this, as here today, that interest will not have to be paid within this 60-day period if taxes have been wrongly declared. The meeting of the Commission asked how much the impact on the state budget, i.e. the financial gain, would be. No specific amount is mentioned in the explanatory memorandum to this draft. It is a measure of a general nature to improve tax compliance and the competitive environment, which has a direct and indirect impact on the growth of public revenue and on cost reductions in the long term. This is part of the government's package of measures to combat tax fraud. The Commission was asked whether the law could not provide for a list of documents which should be submitted in the interests of a faster refund procedure. The people of the Ministry replied that it was not possible to set out a list of such documents in accordance with the general principles of the tax procedure. We also touched on the publication in the Commission of the amount of taxes paid. This proposal, as the Minister of Finance mentioned here, has been made by the Estonian Chamber of Commerce and Industry. Disclosure of taxes paid to the state on the website of the Estonian Tax and Customs Board will hopefully increase Estonia's tax culture and lead to fairer competition. The members of the Commission asked how trade secrets could be protected when these tax data were disclosed. The amount of taxes paid shall be disclosed in aggregate form and the proportion of taxes on labour shall be disclosed in aggregate form within it. This information is general and does not allow meaningful conclusions to be drawn on the financial and working processes of the company. Labour costs are also currently publicly available information through company annual reports. Following these questions and the ensuing discussion, the Commission took the following procedural decisions: At its meeting on 3 June, the Committee on Monetary Affairs decided by 6 votes in favour, 2 against and 1 abstention to propose that the draft be placed on the agenda for the plenary session by 10 June. At its meeting on 9 June, it was decided to propose that the first reading of draft 680 be completed with 6 votes in favour, 1 against, 3 abstentions, and that the deadline for amendments be set at 15 June. There were 5 members of the Commission in favour of this proposal and 4 against it. Thank you!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U591	Thank you. Let's get to the questions. Please, Erki Arrow, second question!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U592	Thank you. Ladies and gentlemen, the rapporteur from the Commission. Was the following subject discussed in the Commission? ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U593	We discussed that this inclusion of 30 days would help the Tax and Customs Board to better investigate, in particular, cross-border tax fraud. The availability of information from third countries within 30 days may be problematic due to their legal space and therefore the Tax Administration sees the need to extend it to 60 days in order to obtain further information from any country; it does not want to label anyone specifically from where the information is to be obtained.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U595	Next is the first reading of the draft Act amending the Income Tax Act, the Social Tax Act and the Estonian Cultural Endowment Act, initiated by the Government of the Republic. I ask for a presentation of the speech chair by Secretary of Finance Jürgen Ligi!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U596	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! I will begin by introducing this draft of amendments which are more important in drawing up the list of non-profit organisations, foundations and religious associations with income tax advantages. In order to simplify and shorten the procedure, the Tax and Customs Board will replace the Government of the Republic. The underlying reason is that listing means implementing tax laws, not making a political decision. The concept of charity of a non-profit organisation or foundation shall be specified. According to the new wording, a charitable association offering goods, services or other goods is mainly free of charge or otherwise non-profit-making or available to the general public. Provision shall be made for more precise conditions for obtaining and maintaining the list, as well as for the requirement that the firm has been active for at least six months before being listed and that it has submitted an annual report. There are also reasons for deletion from the list. The definition of scholarship and the conditions for exemption from income tax paid by listed non-profit institutions shall be defined. The aim is to clarify the distinction between pay and scholarship. The listed companies will be obliged to declare the higher amounts and recipients of scholarships paid during the year and to disclose the information on the tax authorities' website. The draft provides for the taxation of the agricultural and environmental subsidies paid to natural persons by way of income tax, based on the proposal of the Chamber of Agriculture. According to the current law, there is an inequality in the taxation of subsidies, since the benefit paid to a natural person is taxed only if the recipient is self-employed. As a result of the draft, the tax-free rate will increase from EUR 144 to EUR 154 per month, i.e. to EUR 1848 per year. The exemption for crew members, crew members of a public transport vehicle shall be increased from EUR 6 to EUR 10 per person per day for journeys. The daily allowance payable to experts participating in the Twinning or Technical Assistance and Communication project TAIEX, financed by the European Union, shall be exempt from the income tax. The rest of the minor amendments to the Income Tax Act are precise, worded and technical. For example, it is necessary to specify the taxation of the benefit of the sale of the estate due to the recent decision of the Supreme Court. The costs incurred by the seller may be deducted in the future. The law needed clarification. The amendment to the Social Tax Act is technical; the reference to food standards is simply changed. The Cultural Endowment Act is amended to ensure further tax exemptions for grants, scholarships and bonuses paid by the Cultural Endowment of Estonia. The impact of the tax-free income change on the state budget will be 70 million in 2015 and this amount will increase in the future, as certain costs will initially be partly allocated to 2015. As a result of the taxation of the PRIA grants, the state budget for 2015 could receive an additional EUR 1,5 million. By the time the amendments enter into force, we want 1 January 2015. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U636	No more questions for the waitress. Thank you very much, Minister of Finance Jürgen Ligi. Aivar Sõerdi, Member of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, would you like to make a brief presentation?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U637	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The Committee on Finance discussed the draft at 683 meetings, 3 June and 9 June. The first of these was attended by Minister of Finance Jürgen Ligi, and both were attended by the officials of the Tax Policy Department of the Ministry of Finance, Lemmi Oro and Elo Haugas. The purpose of the draft law is to organise the principles and procedures for drawing up the list of non-profit organisations, foundations and religious associations benefiting from the income tax exemption provided for in § 11 of the Income Tax Act, to resolve the problems encountered in the implementation of the law, as well as to clarify the meaning of the law. There is also a need to make changes to the new forms of declaration to be implemented from next year. The draft reduces the tax burden on labour, including the tax burden on low-paid people, as the tax-free rate increases from EUR 144 to EUR 154 per month. The Social Tax Act is amended as a result of the amendment of one of the tax exemption limits in the Income Tax Act. The Estonian Cultural Endowment Act is also amended by amending the Income Tax Act to ensure the exemption from income tax on grants paid by cultural capital. In addition, the Council of the Cultural Endowment of Estonia is authorised to approve the basis for the payment of the prizes, scholarships and grants paid by the Cultural Endowment of Estonia. I will now give an overview of the issues and issues that were discussed in the Commission. There was a question of whether the bill would solve the issue of sports clubs scholarships. The initiator of the draft act explained that the problem was weak control. Scholarships are paid instead of wages and taxes are not paid. These problems have been identified by the checks carried out by the Tax Administration. It was now examined whether the definition of scholarship was given unambiguously and intelligibly. The Commission also considered the acquisition cost of the assets acquired as an inheritance. The initiator of the draft act explained this on the basis of the decision of the Supreme Court. The Court found that there was no provision in tax law to prevent the right to deduct the acquisition cost from being transferred to the heir. At the same time, it has not been dealt with in this way in practice and has been based on the situation that has been the case in practice in taxation. The gift and the legacy should be treated equally. Both recipients acquire property free of charge. Thus, in the case of sales, the benefit is the sale price of the entire transaction resulting from the disposal of the assets. Taxation takes place when the property is transferred, not when the inheritance is received. The initiator of the draft act also explained that personal property is not taxable. The amendment to the Social Tax Act concerning the food standard for the crew was also addressed. The Commission asked whether it should not increase the amount of the external mission limit at all and whether the current arrangements were effective and fair. The prices and salaries have gone up. The principles for drawing up a preferential list of non-profit organisations were also discussed and it was examined why it would be prepared by the Tax and Customs Board instead of the government. The question was also about the impact of the scholarship provisions on how much more taxes will be collected from sports clubs, how and in what amount the compensation will be paid and how many will pay instead of the scholarship. There was a question of the taxation of agricultural subsidies. The initiator of the draft act explained that they were grants due to a shortfall in revenue. No direct tax shall be levied on grants. The Income Tax Act states that the benefits paid under the Act are exempt from tax, but different ministries have drawn up a number of laws that have been documented in various forms of grants. The Commission also asked why it is necessary to amend the Estonian Cultural Endowment Act. The second session discussed more detailed questions on the taxation of inheritances, as well as the taxation by the employer of the provision of aid to persons with disabilities. Once again, clarification was requested on the taxation of agricultural payments. The Commission decided to propose that the draft be placed on the plenary agenda by Tuesday 10 June, from which it was transferred to today. There were 6 members of the Commission in favour, 3 against and 1 abstention. It was also proposed that the first reading be completed, with 6 people in favour, 3 against, and 1 abstention. The Commission proposed that the deadline for submitting amendments be set at 3 p.m. on 16 June. There were 6 members of the Commission in favour, 4 against and no abstentions. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U638	Thank you very much. The question is on Helir-Valdor Seeder.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U640	Twenty per cent of 10 euros remains, you can calculate it yourself, you don't have to ask me. Yes, this global change could have been made earlier, the previous coalition could have been more initiative. In this respect, it covers more than 620000 declarations. Clearly, this is a necessary step to reduce the tax burden on the beneficiaries of the reduced income. And this increase in the tax-free minimum rate is certainly not a definitive one; this will have to be considered in the future.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U643	The tax-free minimum amount per year is EUR 1800 with nothing; I cannot find the exact amount now quickly. It still makes quite a lot of money a year. If you count the monthly amount, it's 12 times smaller.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U646	Got it. First, however, I will give the floor to Helir-Valdor Seeder on behalf of the Group of the Fatherland and Res Publica Union. Eight minutes, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-06-11_U647	Thank you. Ladies and gentlemen, The Act amending the Income Tax Act, the Social Tax Act and the Estonian Cultural Endowment Act is one of the laws of the whole, a package of amendments to the laws with which the temporary government wishes to fulfil the promises of the Coalition Agreement. This includes the amendment of the Road Act, which involves patching up budget holes instead of patching up road holes, and the cancellation of the parental pension until 1 January 2018, which concerns more than 200000 Estonian families, and which was said by the representative of the Government in the Finance Committee that this amendment would not have undesirable effects. But now about the bill we're discussing. First of all, an increase in the tax-free rate from EUR 144 to EUR 154, which the member of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, who had encountered great difficulties before me, gives a victory of EUR 2 per month. In principle, this is certainly the right step, a step in the right direction, which is also supported by the Group of the Fatherland and Res Publica Union, but we do not think that this is enough. In our view, the taxation of people on a low salary of EUR 300-400 is highly questionable, both morally and socio-economically. This could be a cross-party debate, where we would seriously look for solutions so that in the future we do not tax low-paid people who, in most cases, need either municipal or state aid. With the increase in the tax-free rate, it is unclear why the government does not want to legitimise it four years in advance. After all, the increase in the rate of 10 euros has been fixed in the country's budget strategy and confirmed by the government every year until 2018. In the case of excise duties, the behaviour is different: their increase will be fixed by law in the coming years. We believe that the annual increase in the tax-free rate should also be fixed in the law, all the more so since, as I have already said, this has been confirmed by the government in the country's budget strategy. Go on, scholarship theme. That has already been discussed. The desire for equal treatment between sports clubs and cultural institutions is justified, as is the desire for a clear distinction between scholarships and pay. These rules should be understandable and applicable to all. But the definition in the draft doesn't help us even a gram. This is so interpretationable in so many ways, and in fact the tax office will have to analyse each case separately in the future and interpret the law in the light of it. The greatest concern may be that the sport sector is being taken away from just over EUR 10 million a year. It is true that the government has planned to allocate EUR 3.5 million to the Ministry of Culture to cover the scholarships of young coaches, but this does not compensate for the damage suffered by the whole sector, which is more than 10 million. In addition, cultural money, the size of which is very difficult to estimate. We should certainly think about how to ensure a soft landing on the transition. Next, the tax treatment of subsidies with income tax. It doesn't make any sense what the principle of choice has been. For example, the substitute worker's allowance will continue to be subject to income tax, but there will be no breeding aid and no market development aid. All these grants are paid from the budget of the Estonian state. The replacement worker's allowance shall be that of the weakest and weakest farmers. It doesn't make any sense why this choice. Further, payments under the European Union's law on the implementation of the common agricultural policy. They have also been chosen: 21 grants are taxed and a dozen are not taxed. It doesn't make any sense why this choice. This certainly does not mean more equal treatment; on the contrary, unequal treatment is getting worse. I very much hope that we will be able to reach a fairer solution during the draft procedure. And is it even reasonable to tax these subsidies in this form if both the Environmental Investment Centre and the EAS are excluded from taxation and the total receipts of these taxes would amount to EUR 1.5 million per year? A few words about keeping a list of NGOs and taking decisions on them from government level to the Estonian Tax and Customs Board. This is something that has been re-decided several times. Once the Ministry of Finance made decisions, then the government, now they're trying to get it under the Tax and Customs Board. I think that the substance of the matter does not change very much, and so far it was the case that, at the level of the government, several embarrassing proposals from the Ministry of Finance were eliminated. Now this sieve disappears, and I am not sure that it will improve quality. I will come back to the subject of my speech for a moment. My colleague from the Committee on Monetary Affairs said that, thanks to an increase in the tax-free rate, a person should win EUR 1800 a year. No, it's not like that! He said if we divide this number by 12, we get a month's worth. It's not like that! This amount is actually EUR 2 and three pages are devoted to this EUR 2 in the explanatory memorandum. It is said that the amendment of the law '... the aggregated economic and social impact is large. [---] The social impact of the changes is reflected through the higher incomes of the target groups, which extends the potential for consumption and saving for all, but especially for those on low incomes and thus improves coping. Increased consumption has a positive economic impact on companies whose sales revenue is increasing. There is also an indirect impact on the receipts of consumption taxes. [---] Additional economic impact for the target group earning wage income. The impact will be on the overall reduction in the tax burden on labour and the increase in employment in two interlinked formats. " So these 2 euros per month will have a major global impact on the Estonian economy and the people of Estonia, according to the explanatory memorandum. I want to pay tribute to the official of the Ministry of Finance who wrote this paragraph in the explanatory statement. I'm sure he deserves some kind of literary prize. Thank you.
